> 
> And I get people saying, "reckon you'll have another?" And before they even finish their sentence im like, "Absolutely not." 
> 
> I'm 32, and it took me 7 long years to even carry a baby to term, had multiple miscarriages in those 7 years.
> 
> I literally don't have the energy or the strength it in me to do it again. I'm not even sad about it lol. I can't even begin to tell you how extremely easy of a decision it was to NOT have another baby. I got the implant the moment I could after having him lmao.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0330"></a>
**[pbu35-0330]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0330 -->
> I gave birth yesterday via eCS in a private hospital. I was in active labor at 12:36am and it was the most excruciating pain I ever felt in my entire life. I was begging for epidural when I arrived at the hospital at 4:20am. They couldn’t get an anesthesiologist on duty until 2 hours later and all that time I was screaming and crying in pain in the delivery room while my husband stood next to me and didn’t know what to do. So anesthesiologist finally arrived however, I noticed that only the left part of my body felt numb. The right part was still in so much pain that I cried again and wished for death. The anesthesiologist didn’t believe me and I was told that some pain can come and go and that they can’t completely take the pain away so that I can still feel the pressure to push later on when I’m fully dilated. So I endured that for 3 hours, constant pain that I couldn’t explain. They left me screaming in the room with my partner beside me who didn’t know what else to do. The anes had to attend someone else who needed his service and I was told by the OB not to worry because another anes will come and take his place. Then she arrived after an hour. Took her time to chat with the people in the room and when it was time to talk to me, she had a long speech about how epidural should work that I’m supposed to still feel some amount of pain. Then she took on her work equipment and it’s been 4 hours since I had the epidural’s effect on my body. I asked her if it’s possible to check if the epidural is still attached to my back and she said that she will take a look. I was in excruciating pain already all this time. She managed to let me sit on the bed and then found out my epidural had been dislodged. She gave another speech again and there I was screaming and crying in pain while my husband was holding me and then 10 mins later, she finally grabbed the epidural and attached it back on my back.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0331"></a>
**[pbu35-0331]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0331 -->
> I had a fever soon after I got the epidural and lost the strength to respond to anybody in the room. They gave me a paracetamol through the IV fluid. I had to regain some of my strength and then they noticed the baby’s heart rate was almost dropping. My OB immediately asked me to decide whether we wanna still push for a normal delivery or an emergency C-section. I had already lost most of my strength then. I was told they can’t give me another shot of epidural unless I go into a C-section so my planned vaginal delivery didn’t go as planned unfortunately. 
> 
> I agreed to an emergency CS and after baby was out, I heard his first cry. I didn’t get the chance to see my baby so we didn’t have any skin to skin contact. They gave him to my husband who was outside the operating room, and soon after he held him for a few minutes, they put him into NICU because I was told he’s not crying as much. Few hours later, we were told baby’s fine but still need some monitoring so they have to keep him for 3 days.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0332"></a>
**[pbu35-0332]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0332 -->
> Baby’s fine and we’re hoping to get him home tomorrow. I never wanna step into this hospital again and I never wanna give birth again. The pain on my right side that was in constant labor pain for 10 hours out of 13 hours labor is still aching and I’m not sure what to do with it.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0349"></a>
**[pbu35-0349]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0349 -->
> I had one of the same nurses that I had the night before, and while I was in pain she palpated my abdomen to feel for a contraction and said I wasn’t contracting. 5 minutes later the OB on call checked my cervix and said I was fully dilated and that he felt membranes. Homeboy FELT THE SACK AND ASKED FOR LIKE 10 minutes of ultrasounds. After the ultrasounds were done (it felt like forever bc I was in PAIN, plus the nurses kept telling me not to push. Like I’m trying not to?? But my body isn’t listening to me?) they managed to start an IV and ran my bed to the delivery room. Before I was able to move myself to the delivery bed, my water BURST all over my legs. My mom said you could see my son’s head, and that it arched over his head. Once I was on the actual delivery bed, a nurse told me immediately to get on my back (like lady, I’m trying my best here). I felt my son come out, but I didn’t know there wasn’t anyone paying attention until a nurse said “oh, there’s baby!”
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0350"></a>
**[pbu35-0350]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0350 -->
> My baby boy was 3 pounds 8 ounces at birth, and quickly whisked away to the NICU. My boyfriend didn’t think I was in labour, and he had been getting ready for work when my mom called him and said I had our son. (Don’t come for him, neither of us thought I was going to give birth. We were still under the impression that I was a big baby due to my stay the night before, and I had the baby within 30 minutes of getting to the hospital.)  
> It went from the nurses the night before treating me like I had zero pain tolerance, to the nurses the next day acting like I got mauled by a bear and lived to tell the tale.   
> When I thought that wasn’t labor, I was terrified of labor pain. After my son was officially born it didn’t seem so bad anymore.   
> Now I’m adjusting to having to ask permission to hold my son, and not having him at home with me. It’s going to be a long haul for me, but I’m grateful that there weren’t any other complications.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**Further supporting IDs** (including records quoted under another pattern):

[pbu22-0088] [pbu22-0089] [pbu28-0164] [pbu28-0195] [pbu28-0335] [pbu28-0336] [pbu35-0059] [pbu35-0060] [pbu35-0133] [pbu35-0161]

### 18. Recovery can be a treatment workload that competes directly with feeding, sleep, childcare and the ability to move or toilet normally.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 4, 6, 8–10; recovery and first weeks.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Make the first two weeks a full section: feeding, sleep, recovery supplies, the partner's shifts, visitors.”

**What should change or be retained:** Add repeated monitoring, injections, drugs, wound checks, readmission, catheter care, rehabilitation and retained tissue. A six-week clearance or a supplies cart does not define recovery. Explain the parent's requested support and follow-up timing; do not present a medical regimen as advice.

### 19. Feeding, bonding and sleep are shaped by pain, neonatal separation, supply problems and exhaustion rather than by intention alone.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10.

**Relation:** confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.”

**What should change or be retained:** Include inability to touch or feed the baby, feeding pressure and forced-feeding concerns, unsafe-feeling sleep arrangements after every attempted fix, pumping as a competing workload and bond-building that differs by time of day. Do not equate planned feeding, pregnancy attachment or birth route with a guaranteed bond.

[pbu17-0116] [pbu17-0117] [pbu17-0124] [pbu17-0125] [pbu17-0277] [pbu22-0099] [pbu28-0083] [pbu28-0084] [pbu28-0090] [pbu28-0091] [pbu28-0094] [pbu28-0095]

[pbu28-0096] [pbu28-0149] [pbu35-0119] [pbu35-0126] [pbu35-0349] [pbu35-0350]

### 20. The first weeks can bring rage, fear, regret and delayed love, while other parents find joy even after a frightening birth.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 6, 9–10, 17.6.

**Relation:** confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “A healthy baby or an outwardly successful birth does not settle what the mother experienced; relief and improvement also appear.”

**What should change or be retained:** Give the prenatal request for mental preparation its own weight. Keep concealed distress, intrusive thoughts, infant-safety fears, evening deterioration, mental-health treatment and financial barriers. The parent who calls an experience traumatic, the parent who does not, and the parent who is enjoying motherhood are not interchangeable.

### 21. Another pregnancy changes who can rest, attend care, lift a child or give the older child attention.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 9, 10 For 8; personalisation and older-child plans.

**Relation:** confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “The split is practical capacity and previous experience as well as pregnancy number.”

**What should change or be retained:** Include attention guilt even with a supportive partner, chronic sensory load, lifting restrictions after losses, sedation, children's additional needs and absent overnight childcare. The question can be whether to continue the pregnancy, not merely how to schedule labour-day cover.

[pbu35-0265] [pbu35-0322]

### 22. Work and leave can determine whether care is sought, a pregnancy continues, or another attempt is delayed.

**Belongs in:** §§5, 8–10, 16.5, 17.2.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “A calendar is useful to some people but cannot solve the capacity, employment or access problem”

**What should change or be retained:** Add miscarriage-related penalties, firing, work despite severe illness, stopped earnings, threatened dependence, leave promises withdrawn after birth and consideration of abortion followed by trying later to qualify for paid leave. Keep country and employer statements attributed to their authors.

<a id="pbu28-0249"></a>
**[pbu28-0249]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0249 -->
> Not sure what i plan to gain for this rant, but im just feeling very alone rn. I (30F) never wanted kids, and same with my fiance (27M). we found out about me being pregnant fairly early on id say within the 5 to 6 week margin. This is my first time ever being pregnant so idk if i went about all of this the wrong way or not. I definatly told my job considering it is dancing/stripping so I wanted my manager to be aware of why I may not be in as often as well not drinking anymore or smoking anymore.ya know the obvious things you stop when your pregnant. what I didnt realize is how depressed, shitty, feeling like absolute total garbage id feel. especially at night of all things. the hip and joint pain. i mean... you climb up and down a pole for 8 hours with hip pain and tell me youre gonna keep your full tine schedule 😅 i think not. thankfully being an independent contractor youre allowed to make your own schedule.... unfortunately also being an independent contractor mean you have no workers rights. so whenever management seems your work there is done no matter what reason they give you youre essentially fucked. my fiance has been super supportive saying he doesnt want me returning back there and we'll figure everything out. which is nice. but I still feel guilty for even wanting to keep this baby now. I feel so much regret. hes starting to become a lot more comfortable with the idea of knowing who our lil one will be but after this job loss I feel so much regret like if I would have just had an abortion we wouldnt be in this mess. idk.... ive never felt so low in my life before. like ive really fucked up. his family is also trying to be supportive but its like my brain sees the support as being over bearing and I get so annoyed. I should add I also have bipolar depression so this might be why everything feels so intense? some people say its because its my first? idk. i just dont wanna be sad and miserable. I wanna have a happy pregnancy but I cant help to regret going through with this decision especially after getting kicked from my job. just for being pregnant. all of the baby shower dreams I had are down the toilet, all of the gifts that are expensive (ex. babybreeza items, 4 in 1 grow with me bed, baby furniture) I was planning on getting if family couldn't. I think writing this all out i just feel like ive let my baby down before its even here. and its not to say my fiance cant support us, he is a mechanic making ohkay money but I would be lying if I didnt say I make more than him when I do work a full 5 days vs 2 to 4 days. I know being pushed out of my job doesnt make me a bad mom but holy fuck do I feel like such a shitty human being right now.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0441"></a>
**[pbu28-0441]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0441 -->

Was it a good decision?
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu35-0031]**

> 
> I feel so guilty about telling my new supervisor, as there have been cuts in this office too. It is a big department but our unit is only myself and my supervisor. I feel terrible and not sure if I need advice. Maybe just hearing different POV. Thank you!
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0169"></a>
**[pbu35-0169]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0169 -->

There’s a lot he could be doing to help when he’s home without actually taking up much of his time, but no matter how I try and explain that he just doesn’t actually do it. I’ve also been really emotional in general and have been dealing with all of that on my own. We lost our last pregnancy due to a late miscarriage, so I’m dealing with a lot of extra anxiety about this baby just getting here safely, not to mention knowing I’ll have to leave him with either my husband or mom all day when I go back to work after maternity leave and miss so much of his life.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0170"></a>
**[pbu35-0170]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0170 -->

<!-- /verbatim -->

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0319"></a>
**[pbu35-0319]**

>

>

**Further supporting IDs** (including records quoted under another pattern):

[pbu17-0073] [pbu17-0309] [pbu22-0100] [pbu22-0107] [pbu22-0117] [pbu22-0155] [pbu22-0184] [pbu22-0287] [pbu28-0231] [pbu28-0232] [pbu28-0292] [pbu35-0008]

[pbu35-0009] [pbu35-0068]

### 23. A family member, partner or home on paper may be unsafe, unavailable or another person to care for.

**Belongs in:** §§5, 8–10; helpers, housing and the support calendar.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “The visitor calendar alone misses bodily privacy, trust and the mother’s ability to recover.”

**What should change or be retained:** Add caring for a dying or dependent parent, bereavement, unsafe housing, missing water or a clean bathroom, coercive relatives, transport and relocation. Support plans need permission, capacity, reliability and safe conditions, not just names and arrival dates. Preserve actual helpful family and emergency accommodation too.

> But heres what opened up those wounds again. I gave birth, MIL and husband came to the hospital with me. MIL came to stay with us for a week to help out with chores (was not exoecting that at all). She did help out a lot which im grateful for. It did bother me that she changed a lot of things without asking me. And it bothered me that my husband, who would usually talk to me about things, especially changes to our house, was now discussing it with her and only her. And now, things regarding our son, was only discussed with her as well. I was very happy to include her and didnt bother me much until pee got on LO's umbilical cord and i went to ask my MIL for her opinion to make her feel included, decided to get him x,y,z from the pharmacy due to a relative's advice, and she initially agreed with me. I went to husband to tell him, she then changed her mind and aggressively disagreed. My husband agreed with her, took her side, and that opened up this wound. Taking care of my home, the way i want, and taking care of my kid the way i want, and any sort of autonomy, i felt was lost. And this trauma resurfaced. I felt a lack of control. And i felt insecure. So, i turned inward, i crawled back into this shell. I became somewhat overprotective of my son. This heightened my anxiety, made me very worried about every little thing. Just stayed in my room most of the time with my baby. Until she stopped coming.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0095"></a>
**[pbu22-0095]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0095 -->
> A little background: I had planned to raise our baby while finishing my bachelor’s degree online after I just earned my associate’s degree. Then, one month before I gave birth, my 88-year-old mother (she adopted me) with dementia moved in.  
> Now my days revolve around caring for everyone else.  
> I care for our baby full-time. I care for my mother— which includes bathing her, giving her medications, being there emotionally, taking her to appointments & to visit her family every Sunday (even tho I can’t stand them & that alone messes with my mental…. haven’t been around them for at least a decade & I only do it for her). I manage the house, laundry, all cleaning, our dog, doctor appointments, and I spend a lot of energy trying to prevent our baby from crying… AT ALL!!!… because it often sends my mom into a severe emotional meltdown & doing a bunch of weird shit.   
> Meanwhile, my partner works full-time, which I absolutely appreciate, & I don’t want to minimize that, (but my mother has even been able to help with bills since moving in as well so that’s something I’m extremely thankful about even tho… it’s A-LOT let’s be honest no amount of money could amount to the actual workload)….  
> But when my partner gets home, he talks about how exhausted he is, how little sleep he got (even though I survive on naps because I’m up throughout the night & I have a thyroid thing I was tired before baby), how much his body hurts, even how much HES GAINED “BABY WEIGHT”, how much he’s sacrificed by not seeing his friends, (when I’ve sacrificed so much… my body, school, me time—even tho I try so hard to because I know a healthy person makes sure they get it but it’s just not humanly possible rn) & then every day he still makes time to:  
> play Brawl Stars,  
> smoke weed,  
> watch YouTube videos or TV,  
> talk on the phone.  
> He also gets frustrated when our baby looks at the screen & gets fussy cause I won’t just let him! I’ve asked him repeatedly to avoid screen time (from TV to phone) because of the research on early brain development.  
> Sometimes I just wish he’d put the phone down, skip the weed, and be present with us.  
> I don’t even necessarily mean “help more” (although that’s always appreciated… he DOES cook dinner & cut the grass, which is so genuinely appreciated & sometimes when my mother is to much to handle he DOES step in, I give credit where it’s due).
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0121"></a>
**[pbu22-0121]**

> he wants to keep it and I do too but in the back of my mind I don’t because I’m scared, but my family is very very against this I haven’t told them and I have no idea how to tell them and I have no support or family or even friends would give me the time of day and I’m stuck, my family is going to hate me and practically would leave me in the streets homeless they are just so careless, but all my other siblings had kids and my dad has supported them every way, my dad won’t even let my boyfriend stay the night or I stay at his place, he hates the fact I came here, I’m at such a loss and I have no idea what to even do with my life I feel like I’m done for, my dad always told me kids will push me back and I’ll never get to do the things I want like I want to go pro in the horse world and be a helicopter pilot down the road , I don’t know what to do but I’m stuck and I need lots of help..  I could live with them but this means I lose everything I ever loved , my horses my vehicles I won’t be able to afford anything if I get kicked out , I work a full time job already and I make decent amount but I can’t go buy a place

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0337 -->
> So to keep the context short, im 29 F and im 14 weeks pregnant, living with my fiancé at his dad's house until we can save a bit and find a two bedroom. We both work full time and pay rent to be here. Everything started off okay when we first moved in about a month ago, but I recently discovered he has a drinking problem. And when he drinks, not only is he unbearably annoying, but he's also an asshole too. He makes unecessary comments and tries to micro manage everything I do to the point that I cant turn on the sink without him lecturing me about water pressure? Ive had to bite my tongue because I feel like he's doing us a favor but it's honestly making my pregnancy so stressful. On my days off I obviously just want to lay down, and it seems to annoy him for god knows what reason. I still help around the house. He also barges into the one little bedroom that my fiancé and I have, never knocking, almost as if to remind me I have no control here. I used to love his dad until we moved in and I feel terrible for getting to this point but he drinks EVERY single night and yes hes been talked to and he doesnt change. I will stay later at work (my shift every day is 1-10pm and I work in sales at a gym) purposely to avoid having to listen to his drunk banter when i get home. And if i try to avoid him by just going to my room he gets offended. We are planning on moving in a month but how do i handle this until then😭
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0405"></a>
**[pbu28-0405]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0405 -->
> Labor Day weekend I missed a pill during travel but that’s the only time I can think of my dose getting messed up. August 31 I randomly got a migraine (I’ve never had one before) and when googling I read that it can be a sign of pregnancy due to hormone changes. Last Monday I should have gotten my period but didn’t, and I’ve been assuming I’m pregnant since then. I haven’t tested because things were lining up pretty obviously and I’m just waiting for my next paycheck to get abortion pills online because frankly I can’t tell my parents and I’m just trying to get out of this is silently as I can.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0406"></a>
**[pbu28-0406]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0406 -->
> Now today, I started bleeding some. I literally just noticed it so I’m not sure how heavy it is because I caught it starting like 5 minutes ago. Is this a sign of something? I’m googling but there is just too much information in my story that it’s hard to cover my bases. Could my period have just somehow been this late and it’s a coincidence? Is this normal spotting with pregnancy? Is this an early miscarriage? Is this an ectopic pregnancy? I don’t know enough about these different things and I’m freaking out lol. I don’t want to tell my parents and I don’t have insurance so I can’t really do much in terms of asking a doctor.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0407"></a>
**[pbu28-0407]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0407 -->
> I’ve reached the point where I’m constantly checking my body and I’m scared I’ll start experiencing symptoms just because I’m thinking about it so much. My mind has even jumped ahead to imagining being pregnant and needing an abortion, and worrying that my parents could somehow find out through my health insurance.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0039"></a>
**[pbu35-0039]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0039 -->
> i can’t help but feel a bit hurt because we show her two kids so much love for the past 8 years and spoil them whenever they’re in CA to visit. i guess i just expected her to check in on me once in awhile given everything we had to go through. i’m 16w and not once has she asked me how my pregnancy is going… i guess i’m better off not having any expectations or keeping them to a minimum once my son is here. :/
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0040"></a>
**[pbu35-0040]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0040 -->
> She took a lot of time off of work and she just texted that she wants to visit me at work this afternoon to say hi. I am now 20 weeks pregnant with my second daughter. Should I give her a heads up so she can process privately, or just not mention anything unless she does?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0107"></a>
**[pbu35-0107]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0107 -->
> But after I gave birth, while I was barely postpartum late at night he came home completely hammered. I had to clean him up and the floors and pick him up to the bed, all while crying my eyes out holding my newborn. The amount of betrayal I felt was undescribable. I could barely sleep that night.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0112"></a>
**[pbu35-0112]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0112 -->
> We came home late one evening, and I invited my parents over the next day. During their visit, my husband and I were making food for ourselves because we hadn't had time to eat all day.
> 
> I asked my mom if she wanted to hold the baby, and she responded, "You need to do what's best for your baby."
> 
> I told her, "I want you to hold her."
> 
> She seemed very withdrawn and distant toward me.
> 
> After they left, my mom called me and said I was extremely rude for cooking while they were visiting. She told me never to do that again. I became angry, and she hung up on me.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0113"></a>
**[pbu35-0113]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0113 -->
> I understand why my mom was hurt and scared. I understand why she may have felt left out. But I also feel that, as a new mother recovering from a difficult birth and a serious medical condition, I needed support and understanding rather than guilt and emotional distance.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0122"></a>
**[pbu35-0122]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0122 -->
> However, 20 mins later the same child was playing quietly next to her brother and squeezed her squishy dumpling so hard that it broke and SPRAYED IN MY BABY’S FACE AND EYES.  I immediately jumped up and started washing her eyes out under the sink. Baby of course was screaming bloody murder. The in-laws ran to the store to grab eye wash. I have been hearing horror stories  about these squishy toys containing materials that can cause chemical burns so I called poison control and monitored her. The parents’ response was to be like “Oh we’re so sorry” and they left. I continued to flush baby’s eyes for probably the next 30 mins and I finally got her settled. Husband was absolutely sick and angry about the entire situation.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0123"></a>
**[pbu35-0123]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0123 -->
> I think if your kids cause harm to someone else you should probably offer to help or get help and make sure it’s okay. I don’t think it’s cool to just be nonchalant and leave like nothing is going on. This is family, not some stranger who will never see my baby again. They can learn boundaries and rules too. But that’s just my perspective.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0140"></a>
**[pbu35-0140]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0140 -->
> So on the one side I probably know that I’m currently experiencing a miscarriage. But as the fool I am, I also have a slight hope that it might \*not\* be a miscarriage, but a “pregnancy bleeding” - whatever that means.
> 
> I’ve never experienced this before, and to make things worse I just moved to a new country and don’t have a doctor yet, so I don’t really have anyone to call right now AND my partner is away on a business trip. 
> 
> Sorry for the long post, I just don’t know who to talk to or what to do or expect in this situation.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0279"></a>
**[pbu35-0279]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0279 -->
> So I found out I was pregnant and as excited as I wanna be it just is a reminder of what little support I actually have. I am 16 weeks today with a lil boy. 🥳 I haven’t made an announcement anywhere because it really just seems pointless. With my other two kids hardly anyone showed up for any of the special events (baby shower or just coming to see us when we got home). I don’t want to plan a baby shower because my last was pretty pathetic with no games or people who seemed to want to be there at all.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0293"></a>
**[pbu35-0293]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0293 -->
> My partner and I know how difficult it’s been for them to fall pregnant, and the worries that come with early pregnancy for them. We want to send a gift that is less of a “you’re having a baby!” gift, as it’s obviously an uncertain time, but even conceiving is a huge deal so want to send something to celebrate this part of their journey! 
> 
> Something that could be nice and useful now, even in case the pregnancy does not progress the way we all hope. 
> 
> We were thinking a handwritten card, a box of mixed tea bags and maybe a streaming subscription. Nice and useful for early pregnancy and so on. Is there anything else that could be useful during early pregnancy?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0323"></a>
**[pbu35-0323]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0323 -->
> I just feel so sad for him, I just am so confused emotionally right now. It was the first time I had to choose between my future baby and a pet. I’ve worked in animal shelters for years before I got pregnant and don’t just truly breaks my heart. I wanted this pregnancy so bad after suffering several miscarriages in the past and now that I have a perfect healthy baby so far I just feel even more anxious about risking anything.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0356"></a>
**[pbu35-0356]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0356 -->
> Unfortunately, I had a missed miscarriage and had to have a D&C at 12 weeks. It was traumatizing and I have never felt so alone and depressed.
> 
> Now flash forward to now.... I'm pregnant again, by surprise. I'm 11 weeks and so far all my scans are looking good! Baby is growing. The issue is, my poor sister in law just told me she was 4 wks along and had a miscarriage. I am at a total loss. I was already nervous to tell her about this pregnancy after last time. But now, she is so raw and in such a difficult place. I've reached out a few times to check on her. I still haven't even told my family. Should I wait a few weeks or even a month before telling her? Should I delay telling my family as well? Just so they don't mention it to her?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0367"></a>
**[pbu35-0367]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0367 -->
> I’ve been on low contact with my parents too since their main focus was actually my aunt and every other brief update I’ve gave them was mostly a constant comparison to how bad my aunt really has been and that I should be grateful.   
> An example: When I was in the hospital because of slight bleeding my dad’s comment was “Are you copying your aunt?” Since she was also admitted due to bleeding.   
> Also every time I tried to share an update it ended up like “oh your aunt had this”.. “your aunt had that” and when I started sharing less it was “you never tell us anything about your pregnancy! Your aunt keeps us updated all the time but we literally have to pull it out from you.”
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0370"></a>
**[pbu35-0370]**

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0378"></a>

[pbu35-0287] [pbu35-0320] [pbu35-0363] [pbu35-0365] [pbu35-0377] [pbu35-0382] [pbu35-0386]

### 24. Pregnancy can disrupt body identity, gender presentation and eating-disorder recovery as well as change weight.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 6, 9–10; identity, body and personalisation.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Exercise appears as identity, a hoped-for preparation method, a source of guilt and a question about pain or safety.”

**What should change or be retained:** Add androgynous presentation, unwanted feminisation, weight displayed in shared care records, thinness as identity, food restrictions, sensory eating difficulties, small or large bump shame and fear of inadequate gain. Blind-weighing preferences can be defeated by the information system. Keep pride, self-recognition and pleasure in the bump.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu17-0066]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0066 -->

But I hate shopping for clothes so the idea of having to do this multiple times as I get bigger gives me a lot of anxiety so I'd love to minimize the amount of shopping I'll have to do. Should I just go ahead and get maternity pants now and get it over with, or would it be better to just suck it up and get a size up now and have to get maternity pants later?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu17-0213"></a>
**[pbu17-0213]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0213 -->
> I started at 145lbs 5’4, currently 163lbs at 21 weeks, terrified I won’t be able to lose it pp. I’m sorry for everything being weight-focused but during the time I was an obese child (around 160-170lbs in elementary and middle school), my family life was terrible too. Being that weight again just makes me think of those times and how sad I was all the time, I don’t want to project that on my baby when she’s here.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu17-0329"></a>
**[pbu17-0329]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0329 -->
> I'm 14 weeks tomorrow and the baby is a size of a peach, but is it that or the 2 x bowls of pasta and ice cream with strawberries and Nutella? Does the bump look/feel different? 🤣🫣
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0011"></a>
**[pbu22-0011]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0011 -->
> After giving birth I “needed to lose weight” for the first time in my life. At 34 years old, I admit, that’s pretty lucky. But oh my god!!! The obsession that it became!! I was so distraught about my body after birth that I immediately started watching my diet closely and cutting out food groups. I started vaping and taking my adderall prescription again. Anything to get the weight off. And it seemed like nothing worked. I was breastfeeding and not making enough milk (gee I wonder why) and so I stopped that too (also hoping maybe I would lose weight with that). After stopping breastfeeding and basically starving myself for a year, I got back down to 140 lbs. 
> 
> So, was it worth it? Well, it’s pretty obvious. I am embarrassed that I lost my mind like that. It’s absolutely horrible what the expectations are on new mothers. And it’s absolutely horrible to realize that I FELL FOR IT!!! If I would have just taken care of myself and forgotten about what I looked like, I bet the results would have been the same in the end anyway. I hurt my body and I hurt my baby because of the need to “snap back” to my old self. I also didn’t realize how much of my identity that “being skinny” was.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0054"></a>
**[pbu22-0054]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0054 -->
> I have lost all the weight from her pregnancy already. Unfortunately right before her I had a late miscarriage, and I'm struggling to get the weight related to that pregnancy off (about 15 lbs).
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0055"></a>
**[pbu22-0055]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0055 -->

**[pbu22-0105]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0105 -->

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0185"></a>
**[pbu22-0185]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0185 -->
> For moms who are feeling judged when you call your early pregnancy bump a bump (we all feel like we need to say “oh, I know it’s just bloat”)
> 
> You are valid. Your bump is valid. Yes, it’s just bloat, but your baby is very much causing it. 
> 
> Unfortunately, I had a miscarriage this week at 8 weeks pregnant, but I can already see the difference in my body now that my baby is gone. 
> 
> Love your bump, no matter how “bloaty” or small. 🩷
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0193"></a>
**[pbu22-0193]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0193 -->

> 
> I’m exclusively breastfeeding, and so many people told me the weight would just fall off, but unfortunately I’m one of the statistics who has actually gained weight because breastfeeding makes me so hungry!
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0259"></a>
**[pbu22-0259]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0259 -->
> I'm 15 weeks today and have no desire to take bump-date photos like I always thought I would be into. Every video I see online is of these rail thin woman showing off their progress through pregnancy. 
> 
> I feel like I don't look pregnant, I just look kind of fat. I fear my belly won't cohesively round out and be pregnant looking. 
> 
> Logically, I know you need to gain weight for a healthy pregnancy. And since this is something I've wanted, I thought it wouldn't bother me like it does. I had to go ahead and buy some maternity shorts because mine were no longer comfortable to wear and it just makes me want to spiral. 
> 
> I'm also dealing with a weird hip thing that has made staying active hard to do like I planed on. My hip will hurt so bad that I limp whenever it flares up.
> 
> Anyone got any resources that can help me not spiral during this time?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0263"></a>
**[pbu22-0263]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0263 -->
> I have one midwife telling me to take iron supplements for my EXTREMELY low ferritin and the other midwife is telling me that taking iron will screw with my baby’s liver. 
> 
> I’m so fucking tired of this shit. I go to google it tells me not to, other places tell me to take it. 
> 
> I have literally 8.0 ferritin levels. I know I need SOMETHING. I’ve been experiencing severe hair loss as well and my thyroid is all good and I’m on armor thyroid for it. But fuck. I am so tired of misinformation and too much information it just too much sometimes.
> 
> I want the baby to be okay, but I don’t want to hurt her either with the supplements. I even got a good brand called Opti-FerrinC from Pure Encapsulations. 
> 
> I’m just so frustrated.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0034"></a>
**[pbu28-0034]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0034 -->

<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0041]**

<a id="pbu28-0131"></a>
**[pbu28-0131]**

> I’m 5 months postpartum and want nothing to do with sex. My husband and I have had a lot of great bonding moments and were very affectionate. However I truly do not feel sexy whatsoever. I will admit my doesn’t look so different from before. I have always been more on the athletic side. I find my body so gross after having a baby. (It objectively doesn’t look bad) by any means. I just feel gross. It’s my first time and I had a very traumatic birth experience. We were told my baby was most likely going to be a stillborn. My baby is doing great now.. however I feel so asexual now. I don’t find anything or anyone sexy. I know this might sound weird but I’m just so uncomfortable with down there. I at times wish I didn’t have any gentiles at all. I now this is probably very weird thing to post. However I’m wondering if anyone has gone through anything similar to this??
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0135"></a>
**[pbu28-0135]**

Twelve minutes, five pushes and I brought my daughter into this world. These past 13 weeks have been insanity when it comes to the highs and lows I've felt. When I step back and consider what I've accomplished, what my body's accomplished, I'm inspired to believe I'm unstoppable.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0200"></a>

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0239"></a>
**[pbu28-0239]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0239 -->
> This entire pregnancy has sucked, sucked ass. I had horrible morning sickness till about 22 weeks to the point I could barely eat. Then the body dysmorphia when I started showing at 24-25 weeks. I feel like I don’t recognize me at all. I just now start gaining weight, only 3 pounds my entire pregnancy and it was from my 29-31 week appointment. I have low iron (supposed to be at 20 and I’m at 7) so I’m insanely tired all the time and I hate needles so I’m trying the supplements before the infusions. My baby is super healthy but I am not. I don’t resent my little boy for this, I would rather him be healthy than me. He means the world to my husband and I as I had a miscarriage at 12 weeks prior to this. I just do not want to be pregnant anymore, it sucks so fucking much and I feel like no one talks about it. In fact when I talk to my family about being one and done and likely adopting a lovely child who deserves a loving home in the future they always are like “you’ll change your mind” and “you don’t wanna have a little girl?!?”. Like of course I do but adoption of a child who needs and deserves a home and not putting myself through the BS if pregnancy seems like the better option.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0245"></a>
**[pbu28-0245]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0245 -->

The only treatment I've ever received for it has been while pregnant, which has been incredibly challenging. To make things harder, my therapist recently moved away, so I've been without therapy for the last couple of months.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0323"></a>
**[pbu28-0323]**

Like this is just MY fault and not because of the baby, if that makes sense?

Again, I have fished for YEARS and never had this issue. this is also my first pregnancy. I’m almost wondering if it’s because I’m prego or I’m just getting more sensitive over time to certain things.

I feel like I’ve given up so much for this pregnancy and this is just another thing I have to give up when I was already actively trying to eat healthier than I ever have in my life.

**[pbu35-0265]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0265 -->
> I'm looking for honest advice from people who relate.   
> • If you've been pregnant while already overweight, what practical things helped you manage weight gain without restricting calories?   
> • I'm also genuinely curious whether anything I've described sounds like disordered eating or if it sounds more like emotional eating, picky eating, executive functioning struggles, or just years of habits compounding on each other.    
> • Most of all, I'm looking for practical encouragement. What helped you if you hated the gym? If you had sciatica or were very out of shape, what types of movement actually felt doable? How did you learn to enjoy being active—or did you find ways to be active without forcing yourself into traditional workouts?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0305"></a>
**[pbu35-0305]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0305 -->
> I am just over 6 weeks pregnant. I have a history of anorexia and GAD and was diagnosed with ADHD last year. My anxiety and hatred for my body is through the roof. I literally hate how my body feels right now and I just want to sleep for the next 8 months. I feel so self conscious and disgusting. My breasts feel really big and heavy and painful, my stomach is so so bloated and I feel like a huge big lump. I’m terrified that the baby is going to be born with a severe disability which I won’t be able to cope with. I’m scared that I won’t be able to cope with the constant needs of the baby, I’m scared I will be overwhelmed all the time as I don’t like a lot of noise and stimulation. I’m really starting to question if I have made a big mistake. I was in a similar position previously and my mental health got so bad that I had to terminate so I’ve sought support early this time and am getting support from an eating disorder specialist and my GP but I’m not sure it’s enough, I’m just finding it so so so impossibly hard, trying to juggle my full time job at the same time is killing me as it’s quite high pressure and I’m being so unproductive as I’m so tired all the time. I’m just not sure I’m strong enough to carry this baby to term and that is making me so so sad as I’m 38, almost 39 and I really want a family. Has anyone had any similar experiences and made it through?

I did a little research on Norway’s healthcare and found that, as tourists, we would be treated but not covered (by Norwegian health care or our own). Out of an abundance of caution, I’m thinking about travel medical insurance. I’ve never used travel insurance before. Anyone have experience or advice? I don’t feel like reaching out to multiple carriers and getting 10 million calls before I get a quote.
<!-- /verbatim -->

### 27. Pregnancy disclosure and comparison can isolate parents through unwanted sharing, blame, competition or mutual silence after loss.

**Belongs in:** §§3, 5–6, 9; support and what others say.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Family pressure concerns birth choice, vaccines, feeding, body size, risk, and who gets access during recovery.”

**What should change or be retained:** Add protective silence between bereaved and pregnant friends, announcement choices after loss, social pressure toward or against abortion and being treated as only a pregnant body. Friends and relatives can be sincerely supportive and still misread what is needed. Keep privacy and loss in the account instead of reducing this to visitor etiquette.

She got drunk last night and brought it up again after I thought we’d made up and told me it’s ‘weird’ and asked me ‘what’s wrong with her’ whilst crying. My in laws live a lot closer to us (20 min drive, whereas she is an hours flight away) and so her main gripe seemed to be that my in laws would get to see her first as they live so close.

At this point, her reaction is the main issue for me. So just needing some confirmation that I’m not being unreasonable in thinking that me and my husband may want some alone time with our baby before having other people meet her?

### 28. A suspected fetal diagnosis makes the wait a question about the family's future, not simply a request for reassurance.

**Belongs in:** §§4–6, 9–10; screening, diagnosis and decisions.

**Relation:** extends.

**Current conclusion:** “The decision and care experience need their own treatment.”

**What should change or be retained:** Separate screening from diagnosis and the parent's understanding from established fact. Include choices about invasive tests, disability, neonatal care goals, inherited conditions, waiting for specialist explanation and the possibility of a terminal diagnosis. Keep disagreement and fear rather than assuming a diagnosis has already been accepted.

**Verbatim evidence:**

<a id="pbu17-0023"></a>
**[pbu17-0023]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0023 -->
> It has a 50% chance of inheritance due to being an autosomal dominant trait. To say I have been stressed and devastated about my baby having this gene mutation would be an understatement. My excitement feels like it’s gone and has been replaced with pure fear. I spend day and night sobbing, I can’t eat well, I can’t sleep well, I spend hours a day googling and searching for reassurance that never comes.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu17-0050"></a>
**[pbu17-0050]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0050 -->
> I’m relieved to finally have some answers and a treatment plan, but I’m also absolutely beating myself up. I know that ideally, with APS, treatment would have started much earlier, often as soon as pregnancy was confirmed. I’ve been questioning whether I should start baby aspirin for weeks, and I wish I had just done it. I’ve spent months trying to advocate for myself, and yet I feel like I somehow failed to protect this pregnancy by not pushing harder sooner.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0305]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0305 -->
> I advocated for additional testing because I remembered being told when I was younger that I may have Factor V, and the testing instead showed this prothrombin mutation. I was referred to a hematologist.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu17-0343"></a>
**[pbu17-0343]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0343 -->
> Granted. The test haven’t been done yet, lowkey I’ve been holding it off because of how scared I am. **I’m very sick**, on a lot of medications..potentially putting things at risk more….the FEAR is there 24/7. But I feel like it’s a crime to want a bigger family, and it feels like a crime when I told him my side of things of what I can..and cannot handle. Did the way I go about it go wrong? Should I have just kept my mouth shut? And I really the bad guy?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu17-0353"></a>
**[pbu17-0353]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0353 -->
> Still I can’t help but worry for my baby. 20w seems so early to be so small. Has anyone been in this situation? I’m terrified I’m going to lose my baby. Fiancé is convinced everyone will be ok but we weren’t concerned about 1st born this early on…
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0097"></a>
**[pbu22-0097]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0097 -->
> The c section is its own story. But they found that my placenta was partly necrotic. I spoke to the doctor and they have no hypothesis why. I didn’t have high blood pressure for diabetes. And there weren’t enough clots to cause an obstruction. I curious if this would happen again with a future pregnancy.
> 
> Does anyone have a similar story and any answers? I have my own theories about my placenta being done after a week of labor…
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0109"></a>
**[pbu22-0109]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0109 -->
> I’m scared of what my future is going to look like. I dread the future now. The medical issues, the appointments, the therapies, and the possibility of lifelong care. There’s a chance there could be something wrong with the baby’s heart. There’s a spot on it on the ultrasound and while it hasn’t gotten worse since they first spotted it, it also hasn’t shrunk.
> 
> Nobody gets what I’m going through. None of my friends have kids, let alone kids with Down syndrome or illness or anything. No one in my family has any experience with this either. It feels like everyone around me is either guessing or just deciding how I should feel about it.
> 
> My mom has gone all in on it. She’s reading everything she can about Down syndrome, has a whole stack of books, and keeps talking like “this is what it’s going to be” and trying to normalize it. I know she’s trying to help, but I’m not there yet. I just can’t accept it like that right now. 
> 
> I’m struggling with a lot of resentment and guilt at the same time. I feel like a terrible person for not wanting this, but I also feel like I’m being pushed into a life I didn’t choose.
> 
> We’re still together, but it doesn’t feel the same anymore. We’re just two people stuck in the same situation trying to deal with it in completely different ways. She’s scared and worried but she already loves the baby. I’m sorry but I don’t feel that way.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0184"></a>
**[pbu22-0184]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0184 -->
> My midwife I had who explained this to us didn’t even know how to properly explain it. She said she had to call a different doctor at another hospital to get a quick grasp on what to say to us and was sorry because she couldn’t answer any questions. Both my partner and I really just blacked out during the whole thing. We of course have a fetal echocardiogram set up but the quickest they could see us is in 3 weeks. I have heard that is reassuring as if it was something really bad they would make time as soon as “possible” to see us but I am just so torn. I don’t know if I should do genetic testing because what if my baby has some sort of gene sequence missing or an extra chromosome and then what?.. or it could go the other way and give peace of mind. I haven’t shared the thought with my friends or family that I feel absolutely god awful thinking my alcohol consumption/no pre natal vitamins had something to do with it. This whole pregnancy has been a waiting game as our insurance for my partners new job didn’t kick in til the first of may so our first pre natal appointment was very late compared to the normal time frame. I was so looking forward to this scan for reassurance and to finally be able to get excited for the gender reveal/baby shower and my worst fear came true. I just feel so weird and so bad and we are okay finically but no where near comfortable to deal with a ton of medical bills. Is anyone else currently dealing with this? Would you do genetic testing? I’m trying not to look up a lot of stuff online and go down the rabbit hole… thank you for reading.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0238"></a>
**[pbu22-0238]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0238 -->
> So far, \*\*my blood pressure has been completely normal\*\*, and my \*\*NT scan came back with reassuring results\*\*.    
> Has anyone had a healthy pregnancy after severe early-onset preeclampsia? What are my chances of having a healthy pregnancy and bringing home a healthy baby this time?
> 
> \*\*Also, can monitoring my blood pressure from the beginning of pregnancy help prevent preeclampsia from happening again through treatment, or does it only help detect it early?\*\*
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0246"></a>
**[pbu22-0246]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0246 -->
> However, now that I’m back on the monitor, a nurse noticed that babies heart rate is actually double what it’s saying on the NST and that the bands aren’t reading the double beat, so they grab an ultrasound machine and yeah babies heart rate is actually at 260-280 consistently and not slowing down or going back to normal. 
> 
> They’ve given me one steroid shot for his lungs incase we have to induce early, they’re keeping me overnight to continue monitoring and to figure out what medication to try to get his heart rate to settle down. I’m so scared that medicine won’t work and that we’ll have to induce and be in the NICU.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0251"></a>
**[pbu22-0251]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0251 -->
> My midwife sent me home with a blood pressure cuff around 30w or so and I realized I was struggling with high blood pressure like all the time (genetic on my dads side). The midwife sent me to an ultrasound and we found out at 36+4 that the baby was measuring really small, she compared him to a 26 weeker. I ended up at the ER and was diagnosed with late onset preeclampsia at 37w and got an emergency C-section. My son was born 2lbs 11oz and the whole experience was very traumatic. Baby spent 5 weeks in the NICU which was also traumatic for all of us. \*\*I also didn't end up receiving any postpartum support from the midwife and never got a refund for the 5k my mom paid her!\*\*
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0254"></a>
**[pbu22-0254]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0254 -->
> Finally get off bed rest just to be told there is a blood type incompatibility (little-c antibody and high possibility of my baby developing hemolytic anemia). After this diagnosis I was referred to a high risk MFM specialist who will be monitoring the baby but also told me I have a marginal chord insertion and that this could create even more issues. They have given me a goal of making it to week 35 and then a likely induction. I feel like after all this my excitement is gone and I wake up every morning feeling dread. I have so much anxiety and the little kicks and flutters I was so excited to feel just remind me of the precious boy inside me who my body is just constantly failing in every way. I’m full of self loathing and I just don’t feel any connection to the baby or the pregnancy and I think it’s because I’m just so scared I could lose it all any moment. Both my other two pregnancies were totally normal and healthy so even though TTC took a while in didn’t anticipate issues after that part of it.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0260"></a>
**[pbu22-0260]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0260 -->
> We got a call from the pediatrician at 3:50pm on Friday (how nice!) where he said our son may have cystic fibrosis due to an elevated IRT of 55.7. Now he said it’s just a screening test, NOT a diagnosis. So we have to go to do a sweat test next week to confirm. I am spiraling, I am an absolute mess.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu22-0284"></a>
**[pbu22-0284]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0284 -->
> Has anyone here been referred for suspected bladder exstrophy because the bladder couldn’t be visualized, but it ended up NOT being exstrophy? Or, if your baby was ultimately diagnosed with it, what did the prenatal ultrasounds show and when were you able to get a definite diagnosis? I would also really appreciate hearing from parents of children with bladder exstrophy. I know Google can make everything feel terrifying and right now I think I need to hear from real families about what life actually looks like for these kids.  
> I’m trying not to get ahead of myself before we see the specialist, but as his mom, it’s really hard not to be scared. ❤️
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0012"></a>
**[pbu28-0012]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0012 -->
> Of course I’m worried about my baby and what this could mean if she does possibly have down syndrome. Me and my husband had a brief conversation and decided we’re still going to go through with the pregnancy, but we’re still going to do some research about it and wait for the second results to fully make out decision. It’s just a lot to take in and we’re both very scared of all of it.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0122"></a>
**[pbu28-0122]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0122 -->
> My baby’s head is in the 2nd percentile.. been referred to a specialist. I am beyond terrified and after finding out what it can mean to have a HC so low.. have barely stopped crying since the scan, this was 5 days ago and I still haven’t been back to work or left the house. Sonogropher stated that small HC can mean Microcephaly.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0123"></a>
**[pbu28-0123]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0123 -->
> Leaving this link here (for any mums worried about a similar thing) I was told by FM that if the GA is even a few days out it can make a BIG difference. Example: NHS has your GA at 20 Weeks but if you know the EXACT date of conception and know your actually 19 Weeks +2 days the measurements can COMPLETELY change the percentile. Unsure about my personal date of conception but it brought me so much relief to see the percentile/dates/MM measurement differences as there’s really not much between them!
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0194"></a>
**[pbu28-0194]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0194 -->
> The doctor wants me to get a mammogram and ultrasound just to check on everything but I’m horrified. There was no lumps or anything visually wrong but she wants to be safe, which I understand and I also want to make sure nothing is wrong but I’m so scared as well. I’ve been crying since the appointment and I just don’t know what to expect or what to do and I wish I didn’t have to deal with this on top of everything.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0214"></a>
**[pbu28-0214]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0214 -->

I sent an email asking the doctor why i need fetal echo. I havent heard back yet but is this normal for ivf pregnancies? Should i be concerned?
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0215"></a>
**[pbu28-0215]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0215 -->
> I know everyone’s situation is different, but I’m really looking for success stories from women who had severe shortening/funneling with a cerclage and still made it much further along. How long did your cerclage hold? Did anyone stabilize after things looked bad? Just looking for honest experiences because my anxiety has been through the roof.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0244"></a>
**[pbu28-0244]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0244 -->
> Hi yall,   
> After trying to get pregnant for two years, 2 miscarriages and 6 medicated cycles we finally got pregnant with what would be my second child! We decided to do the NIPT test, and with my first born it took 7 days to get the results back.   
> With this round it took my OB 6 days to submit my test to Myriad.   
> Which then they took almost 2 weeks because they said my sample needed to be reprocessed.   
> Anyway. After almost a total of 3 weeks in total from my blood drawn I wake up to my test in the clinical review portion. I was thrilled. That thrill was short lived. My OB office called me and told me unfortunately my test was inconclusive and they recommend me to see a maternal fetal specialist and speak with a genetic counselor.   
> The genetic counselor told me that my test wasn’t inconclusive due to BMI or low fetal fraction. They asked if I was on blood thinners which I said no. So then they said because it wasn’t a low fetal fraction for the cause a lot of the time it means there’s an abnormality with the baby. Which is why they want to me see a maternal Fetal specialist. I started sobbing. I read Reddit stories but everything I read was due to low fetal fraction I haven’t found a story of it being inconclusive for another reason other than the last option which is there being an abnormality im so stressed and can’t stop crying. I’m scared and since it’s Friday the Maternal fetus specialist said they’ll call me next week to set up an appointment so now I’m in a limbo of unknown.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0248"></a>
**[pbu28-0248]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0248 -->
> My wife went to ER over the last few days and they told her she had low amniotic fluid and it was very likely leading to a miscarriage. ER said they can’t do anything before 20 weeks and we will have wait for our OB. Does anyone have any advice for helping with fluid? Just bed rest and hydration until we get appointment? Any advice will help. Thank you
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0250"></a>
**[pbu28-0250]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0250 -->
> I am really just looking for any solidarity. Has anyone found out they were a carrier for a dangerous gene? How do you cope with the guilt and fear? I feel unbelievably alone right now.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0251"></a>
**[pbu28-0251]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0251 -->
> I'm so scared that the ventricle will have increased in size and what that would mean for me, my baby, and my husband. I'm also very upset because by the time I get the results, I'll be 32 weeks pregnant and only about five weeks away from delivering her early because of her low percentile.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0252"></a>
**[pbu28-0252]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0252 -->
> I know that the most likely outcome is probably isolated ventriculomegaly, with no specific adverse effect on my baby's development, but I'm worried sick. I've found this whole pregnancy emotionally difficult, especially after having a miscarriage before. I thought this pregnancy was going well and that I could finally start to relax and get excited. Instead, I feel like I've been punched in the face. And that I got ahead of myself with all the baby prep. Feels like a punishment.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0284"></a>
**[pbu28-0284]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0284 -->
> I'm not looking for false reassurance, just honest experiences while I wait for the fetal medicine appointment.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0322"></a>
**[pbu28-0322]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0322 -->
> I’m currently on kid number 4 and I’m 29 and 4 days. My blood pressure was semi high at my last appointment at 139/76. I was preeclamptic with my first but only for like 3 or so days before I was induced and delivered. I’m currently itchy as hell all over with no rash and super uncomfortable to the point I’m maybe sleeping 4ish hours a day. I have my next appointment on the 22nd and have already spoken about what would happen if I am preeclamptic and told they would induce me at 34 weeks??!! My second kid was in the NICU for 2 days and it really messed me up for months. I’m terrified of delivering so early and if I do my husband and I agreed maybe it should be our last considering my last two kids were 36 and 4 days. I’m for sure going to ask more questions about what to do because it seems the aspirin is not doing the full job? What helped you get through having a NICU baby? Im struggling really bad with just the thought of it already. I have a feeling she’s going to be early no matter what.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0345"></a>
**[pbu28-0345]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0345 -->
> Just had a brutal 20 week scan today where the MFM specialist told me that my son is likely to have severe neurodevelopmental disabilities when he is born due to several brain abnormalities.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0346"></a>
**[pbu28-0346]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0346 -->
> Came home exhausted. Husband fell asleep after holding me crying on and off all day. Then one of my lovely cats came and cuddled me and man even though it doesn’t change anything I just felt SO COMFORTED. That was my glimmer of hope that we might be ok amidst the mountain of uncertainty ahead of us.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0374"></a>
**[pbu28-0374]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0374 -->
> At the end of the day our baby girl is here, we are here, she’s alive and we’re going to live our life together no matter how long that is. Prior to our daughter being born my husband was a workaholic, no matter how many hours he put in or what his check looked like he never thought it was enough, everything came down to his goal of buying a house which in this day and age feels like a constantly moving goal post. We’ve now accepted that a house isn’t our goal, working 70+ hour weeks will never be worth it when we’ve got a limited amount of time with our daughter.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu28-0375"></a>
**[pbu28-0375]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0375 -->
> Instead of spending our days stressing about “are we doing what we need to in order to create someone who can function on their own in this world” we get to worry about making every day as happy and enjoyable for our girl as possible. Obviously this situation sucks, but, it has reframed parenthood for us, we truly get to live every day just worried about spoiling our daughter and I think that’s a beautiful thing.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0395]**

<a id="pbu35-0007"></a>
**[pbu35-0007]**

> 
> Of course the thought of re-experiencing the same issues — especially now with a child to care for — is really weighing on me and I'm wondering if preventive TAC might help minimize this risk.

<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0009"></a>
**[pbu35-0009]**

I’m also very scared of wasting all that money on IVF and not having that work - I think that would make me feel like it was impossible and we would only be able to afford the one round.

<a id="pbu35-0053"></a>

>

>

>

<a id="pbu35-0090"></a>

<a id="pbu35-0134"></a>
**[pbu35-0134]**

<a id="pbu35-0135"></a>
**[pbu35-0135]**

<a id="pbu35-0179"></a>
**[pbu35-0179]**

>

> I am 40 years old and  pregnant(suprise pregnancy). I have had two inconclusive NIPT test. Negative Quad screen and negative NT measurement. She recommends an amniocentesis based primarily on the two inconclusive NIPT test and my maternal age of 40.  I wondering if anyone has been in this situation and went through with the amnio? My husband wants me to skip the proceducre due to risk of miscarriage. Any advice would be appreciated. The MD made it sound like two inconclusive NIPT results had strong indication for genetic abnormalities.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0200"></a>
**[pbu35-0200]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0200 -->
> I asked her how much my chance for a successful pregnancy went down since everything and she just said   
> “Mmm, you know with every pregnancy there’s about a 20% chance of miscarriage but you’re able to get pregnant, we’re taking your blood HCG, and then doing a viability scan in 3 weeks” 
> 
> and that’s really it… I read in the what to expect before expecting book that after two losses there’s a 75% chance of a successful pregnancy and then I just stopped reading because it freaked me out.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0203"></a>
**[pbu35-0203]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0203 -->
> I am so scared of pre-eclampsia. My mother didn't have it, only my great-great-grandmother had it. I feel a bit helpless. Has anyone had experience with pre-eclampsia? How was it for you? And how was your delivery?
> 
>   
> Thank you in advance. I hope you can be nice, I don't know where else to go because I'm the only one in the family (and among friends) that's been diagnosed this.
<!-- /verbatim -->

<a id="pbu35-0205"></a>
**[pbu35-0205]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0205 -->
> Hi!!! So i just found out today at my first ultrasound that im pregnant with twins at 5w2d! I’m very blessed and so excited the only thing is that one is measuring at a different gestational period than the other. They are literally 2 days apart, even my doctor was kind of speechless. He said there is a possibility that the smaller one is a “vanishing twin”. I’m kinda nervous on what that means, i’ve been ttc for 3 years and have 4 miscarriages so when i hear i might be loosing one twin it’s really scary. 
> 
> I guess what i’m trying to ask is if anyone else has experienced this??? how does this work? Like does the bigger twin absorb the other one like i always hear about or is much worse than that? I do understand this is apparently pretty rare and based on genetics i’m just looking for a little support for the ones that have gone through it <3
<!-- /verbatim -->

> 
> I see a different nurse every time I go to the OB, and the woman I saw that day said that it was normal, except the right side of the baby’s heart was dilated, but it was hard to tell since they haven’t fully seen the left side of her heart and a fibroid was obstructing some of the view (first time I’ve ever been told I have a fibroid). she didn’t seem super concerned, but that didn’t ease any of my nerves.

They said to call on Monday to schedule time to come in and discuss monitoring more frequently. And I assume they’ll probably talk about the possibility of delivering earlier depending on how labs look. 
>

> I got back up to my room and they tell me they want to do an amnisure test again. They did 2 this time. \*\*Both came back now positive.\*\* The nurses came in and said “we’re so sorry but you won’t be going home for quite some time. We are moving you to long term pregnancy floor.”     
> I get moved, and this nurse tells me “you’re not leaving at all, just be aware.”     
> My pregnancy has been textbook \*\*“perfect”\*\* she’s been growing right on track, I’ve only gained 17lbs, blood pressure has been amazing, no swelling, no complications or major complaints besides just being uncomfy. So to say this was a SHOCK. I felt like a punch to the gut. All my plans, out the window - maternity pictures that were next week, our matching mommy and me outfit, her birthing announcement outfit, i kept thinking “we were supposed to have 7 more weeks together. Just us.”
<!-- /verbatim -->

> Organizing     
> Getting the bottle station set up 
>

Now, I just feel sad and guilty for having sex. Had we not, I wouldn’t have bled. I am so heartbroken at this entire situation and feeling an immense amount of stress, guilt, shame.
<!-- /verbatim -->

[pbu17-0051] [pbu17-0193] [pbu17-0308] [pbu17-0348] [pbu22-0291] [pbu28-0051] [pbu28-0098] [pbu28-0283] [pbu28-0450] [pbu35-0084] [pbu35-0230] [pbu35-0260]

[pbu35-0274] [pbu35-0284]

## Outliers and counter-examples

These are counter-evidence to a universal reading of the patterns, not exceptions to be explained away. Their recurrence in this selected share is not a frequency estimate. Claims about medical safety or outcomes remain attributed testimony.

### Checking is not always fear, and little checking can be enough.

**[pbu22-0283]**

I’m just over 6 weeks now and had my first ultrasound today. Everything is tracking well and we saw the heartbeat. My husband and I nearly cried from relief and joy 🥹

**[pbu22-0292]**

I feel like this is a good sign that I’m healing and putting that episode behind me. I hope this gives hope to others that have had pregnancy scares, it really does get better! 🩵🩵

Unsure about my personal date of conception but it brought me so much relief to see the percentile/dates/MM measurement differences as there’s really not much between them!
<!-- /verbatim -->

### Good partners and fathers' suffering both challenge an absent-helper stereotype.

**[pbu17-0054]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0054 -->
> My partner and I have been together for 6 months. We both know this is it and have spoken about marriage, etc. he is supposed to move in with me in September. He has been incredibly supportive. I know he’d prefer if I had the baby and was excited when we found out. Despite this, especially after seeing the toll this has taken on my mental health, he is backing me 100% whatever I choose.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0126]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0126 -->
> Two weeks is barely enough time to understand what has happened, let alone recover from the birth, support your partner, learn how to care for a newborn and deal with complications when they happen. I'm incredibly grateful that I've been able to be here with my wife and son, but going back to normal life after two weeks feels incredibly difficult when your family is still finding its feet.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0127]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0127 -->
> And strangely, despite how difficult these two weeks have been, I feel like we've come out of them loving and respecting each other even more.
> 
> I've seen my wife in pain, exhausted and vulnerable, and I've watched her still put our baby first. She's seen me struggling to figure things out too, and somehow we're learning to work as a team in a completely new way.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0128]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0128 -->
> Some people asked how her and I were doing. I had some people reach out to me and wish well for just her. It was over three months before I had someone pull me aside, another man who had experienced similar loss, and say, 'But how are YOU doing?'... 
> 
> &#x200B;
> 
> And I felt seen in a way that I didn't know I needed to be.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0240]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0240 -->
> My partner is sooo gracious, patient, understanding, supportive, reassuring, encouraging and nurturing to me through all this and he’s the reason I’ve been able to become the soft, warm, tender, vulnerable, nurturing, receptive version of myself that I am continuing to embody, embrace & reclaim more & more..
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu17-0321]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu17-0321 -->
> The first two transfers failed but the third one was successful! 
> 
> It's been such a long, stressful, painful, and emotional road. But we're finally here and I couldn't be more thankful for our next chapter!
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu22-0007]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0007 -->

I used to handle the domestic duties, and he's not as clean as me (or as I used to be) so laundry doesn't get done quickly and clutter is around more. I just shut down when I attempt to clean or tidy up. I don't have clean freaking clothes so I just stay in my pjs all day. I appreciate all my husband does but the work is still piling up and I feel like I'm drowning.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu22-0111]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0111 -->
> I’ve already been working on a list to ask the obgyn because she’s going to be a high risk pregnancy, so I have that front covered but will still definitely be reading and educating  myself as much as possible. I will  also take notes while we are at the appointments as well.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu22-0232]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu22-0232 -->
> I know she’s the one making the baby and she’s going through a lot but this is very stressful for me too and I’ve been putting on a brave face and talking her through everything she needs to talk about but in reality I’ve been sitting on the bathroom floor with the door locked having a panic attack every other night and just not saying anything to her because I don’t want to stress her out even more. I see so many posts of women saying they freaked out on their husbands their whole pregnancy and laughing about it like it was a big joke but I don’t find that funny. We’re supposed to be a team and I know I’m not the one going through the physical challenges but I’m working hard to provide for the family that we’re soon to have and working 60+ hours a week to make sure we’re set up for an easy transition into our new life while being constantly verbally berated when I don’t believe I’ve done anything wrong is taking a serious toll on me already.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0155]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0155 -->
> I did it because I knew this was a once-in-a-lifetime moment. I realized that years from now, if my wife wants to look back and remember the exact details of how our daughter came into the world, nobody else will be able to tell her that story except for me. I wanted to be her memory bank for that day.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0156]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0156 -->
> Right after birth, they had to take my wife away for stitches. Because of that, I got to hold our little girl for the entire first hour—our golden hour. Just skin-to-skin, just the two of us. After that day, for months, the only way my little sweetheart would fall asleep was lying right here on my chest.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0157]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0157 -->
> Wife and I welcomed a beautiful baby girl last week. It was an extremely traumatic birth, with my wife having an actual near death scare. I can’t bring myself to put all the dark/difficult details into words, but it does end with she and I coming home with our baby girl and everyone is doing just fine. But I can’t shake the anxiety - I’m having trouble eating and sleeping, am experiencing intense chest pain, and am in a state of panic most of the time. I feel like I am already failing in keeping my wife and daughter safe and secure even though I know logically that isn’t true or fair to myself. I have already reached out to a therapist and made a dr appointment to talk about anxiety meds. I guess I’m experiencing post partum anxiety?
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0158]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0158 -->
> Yeah, she’s been amazing. I feel so bad because I should be taking care of her right now! But she would say that I am taking care of her. And she was the one who talked me into talking to my doctor about meds and has been so encouraging through this whole thing.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu35-0135]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0135 -->
> I’ve taken two personal days off from work, I’ve been ringing round and emailing all Down Syndrome charities locally and nationally. That’s how I deal with things, I deal with the practicalities, often to the detriment of being emotionally there for my partner, I won’t let that happen this time.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu35-0153]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0153 -->
> This morning, I went in for a second opinion. Not because I didn’t believe what I had already been told, but because I needed my husband to fully understand it the way I did. I needed him to hear it from the doctors, to ask his own questions, and to be just as clear and grounded as I was about what was happening and what I was about to endure.
> 
> And he did exactly that.
> 
> He was present, engaged, asking thoughtful questions about my body, about the timeline, about what this meant and how he could support me through it. I needed that from him. I needed him to see it, not just hear it from me.
> 
> But if I’m being honest, I needed it for myself too.
> 
> We had now seen three doctors. This second opinion was the most thorough. They walked us through everything.

**[pbu35-0386]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu35-0150 -->
> She was 8lbs 15 oz, much bigger than my other two babies who were about 7.5 lbs each (and early). I did tear a little and got 2 stitches, but the midwife said it was really minimal. > 
> It was so nice to already be home. The midwives helped us get cleaned up, situated in our bed, started laundry, then checked all baby’s reflexes and everything else for the Apgar score right there on the bed (she passed with flying colors).
> 
> Then they left us to rest and bond in bed together, and we didn’t sleep a wink because we were so busy staring, admiring her little features and gushing over her tiny little adorable noises.
> 
> Having such a fast labor was intense but the whole time I knew I could do it. I didn’t use any affirmations even though I had prepped some, but one of my favorite things I kept thinking was; it was all a team effort between me and my baby. <!-- /verbatim -->
