> I want to explain something about how I’m processing all of this, because I sometimes feel as though my experience doesn’t quite fit the way miscarriage is usually talked about. I don’t feel ashamed to say that the experience has been traumatic, but I don’t feel as though I’m grieving the loss of a baby in the way some people describe. It was so early that I never saw anything on a scan beyond the gestational sac. I imagine that seeing a heartbeat or more of the pregnancy developing might have made it feel more real to me. It feels as though I’d only just processed the shock of finding out I was pregnant when we were told it probably wasn’t going to continue. There was barely any time to get my head around the idea of being pregnant before I was dealing with the uncertainty and reality of losing the pregnancy. 
> 
> I’m at peace with the fact that this pregnancy wasn’t meant to continue, but that doesn’t mean the experience itself hasn’t been difficult.
<!-- /verbatim -->
## What fits nowhere

These topics lack a sufficiently explicit home in the current findings. Some can become named subsections inside existing sections; others require broadening the reader and the stage arc. Cross-references here do not remove their full words from the evidence.

### Loss and its management need a named place in the stage arc.

The existing arc proceeds toward live birth and first weeks. Miscarriage management, ectopic loss, induction for stillbirth, care after a loss and trying again require their own transitions. A date such as viability or the exact weekday of a previous loss can matter more than trimester.

### Continuation, abortion, adoption and changed decisions need a named place before the guide hypothesis.

Keep these as research evidence even when the speaker is not choosing to parent, is a minor, has not confirmed pregnancy or changes course after starting an abortion regimen. They cannot be inferred to be guide customers. The adolescent account makes adoption and attachment concrete; failed or incomplete abortion accounts need their own route into care.

### Coercion needs its own category rather than only the partner-help section.

Pressure can be to continue or to end pregnancy. The reported control concerns bodily decisions, housing, transport, money and confidentiality. Explicit threats of suicide belong in their reported coercive context, rather than being detached into a generic mood category.

### Disclosure, legitimacy as a parent and child-protection fear need explicit treatment.

Keep the difference between a reported actual contact and a feared consequence. The fear of losing a baby or care can obstruct disclosure of medication, substance use, suicidal thoughts or trauma even when the person already has clinicians or a therapist.

### Identity needs room for gender presentation and the design of care records.

Pregnancy is experienced as unwanted feminisation in one account, not merely dissatisfaction with size. Another parent cannot maintain blind weighing because the shared health card requires a visible weight field.

### The first weeks may be a hospital, separation or end-of-life family period.

The ledger describes a terminal neonatal diagnosis behind the changed-goals passages. Keep that context alongside repeated admissions and restrictions on a partner staying overnight; an ordinary home-recovery checklist cannot represent these situations.

**[pbu28-0095]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0095 -->
> Shortly after his birth, he had many health issues. Every week he was diagnosed with a new disorder. Each and every time my heart broke. Whenever I finally accepted the new diagnosis, another one came up. It‘s been 6 weeks since then and I haven‘t had a single happy memory of becoming a mother.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0096]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0096 -->
> We were home only for a few days the rest of the days were all spent in the hospital. What‘s worse is that my husband was never allowed to stay with us. Which means I‘ve been taking care of my baby all alone for 6 weeks and only get a break for 4 hours during visitor times.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0097]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0097 -->
> I‘m not gonna back out or leave my son, I would never. I‘m gonna get a grip and struggle for years to provide him a good life but I hate it. I won‘t enjoy it and I will carry this regret for the rest of my life.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0374]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0374 -->
> At the end of the day our baby girl is here, we are here, she’s alive and we’re going to live our life together no matter how long that is. Prior to our daughter being born my husband was a workaholic, no matter how many hours he put in or what his check looked like he never thought it was enough, everything came down to his goal of buying a house which in this day and age feels like a constantly moving goal post. We’ve now accepted that a house isn’t our goal, working 70+ hour weeks will never be worth it when we’ve got a limited amount of time with our daughter.
<!-- /verbatim -->

**[pbu28-0375]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0375 -->
> Instead of spending our days stressing about “are we doing what we need to in order to create someone who can function on their own in this world” we get to worry about making every day as happy and enjoyable for our girl as possible. Obviously this situation sucks, but, it has reframed parenthood for us, we truly get to live every day just worried about spoiling our daughter and I think that’s a beautiful thing.
<!-- /verbatim -->
### Scope-edge and low-context passages stay visible without invented context.

A professional asks about abortion-doula training; this is a service-provider request, not a parent's purchase intent. Several records describe fear before pregnancy is confirmed. Very short fragments cannot carry a precise diagnosis, product identity or conclusion independently. These remain evidence with those limits.


**[pbu28-0067]**

<!-- verbatim:pbu28-0067 -->
> Hi everyone! I am a social worker and former public defender and I’m interested in becoming a certified abortion doula. It’s something I’ve been thinking about for years, ever since I learned it was something that existed. I admire birth doulas and full-spectrum doulas hugely, but abortion work is something I’ve always wanted to get back into and this a way of doing it that really aligns with my values. I realize it will likely be mostly volunteer work unless I’m somehow able to form some kind of partnership with a clinic and I’m okay with that as I am currently employed. I have been doing a little bit of research and I came across Rouge Doulas, which was recommended to me as a starting point for training, as well as Allo Doula Academy, BirthPro Full Spectrum Training, and The Lemon Grove. Does anyone have experience with any of these or recommendations for any other training programs? Thanks in advance!
<!-- /verbatim -->

Everytime I do anything seuxal with my boyfriend I feel so utterly guilty, like I just broke all the values I hold close to myself. ### Provenance and limits that must travel into the rewrite

The ledgers preserve same-author and crosspost limits. In particular, the accounts quoted as [pbu35-0090] [pbu35-0092] are linked by the UR35 ledger to the same archived author; one account describes a stillbirth and another uses neonatal-loss classification. Retain that difference without resolving it by inference. No quote count here is a count of distinct parents.

The Eqqual Berry purchase [pbu17-0261] remains an actual reported purchase but sits against the existing findings’ warning about an Eqqual Berry promotional cluster. The named Dimora dressings in [pbu28-0401] also resemble the product-post wording already flagged in the findings; that is a provenance caution, not a fresh verified seeding finding. Keep both records and their words; do not use either as independent evidence of efficacy or brand value. Named services and medications are not all purchases. No source in this share demonstrates a purchase of the proposed Mamivio guide.

Later-postpartum records describe some consequences and retrospective birth experiences; do not silently relabel every such event as occurring in the first two weeks. Adoption, abortion-doula training and unconfirmed-pregnancy questions remain visible at the scope boundary. They do not automatically define a customer segment.

## Money

This index includes stated amounts, actual or contemplated purchases, gifts and acquisitions, refunds, willingness to spend, unpriced care, inability to pay, income and financial control. A private service or an item already owned is not automatically a confirmed paid purchase. Read the complete field under the linked ID for all conditions and amounts. None of these prices values the proposed Mamivio guide.

### How the money changes the current conclusion

The current §10 statement “The money stress is mostly bills, daycare and leave” needs an explicit extension: money also affects continuation, adoption considerations, delaying another attempt, an escape from an unsafe relationship, diagnostic certainty, relief and recovery treatment. Paid-leave ineligibility enters the decision about abortion and trying later [pbu28-0440] [pbu28-0441]; refusing admission can be an income decision [pbu17-0042]; a missing refund is attached to alleged absent postpartum care [pbu22-0251]; and trauma treatment is a further family expense [pbu22-0098]. Conversely, [pbu17-0316] [pbu17-0340] explicitly put relief or prompt care above price. These are distinct purchasing situations, not one price ceiling for information.

## Requests

### Product, plan, list, service, treatment and action requests

The index deliberately keeps the object of the request specific. It includes direct questions, requests already made to professionals, proposed requests, and stated unmet preparation needs. A request to explain a test, provide treatment, help with a decision, find a prescriber or manage a symptom is not a request to buy a trimester guide. The professional training request is identified separately below. Full original fields are under their IDs; these locator descriptions do not substitute for the parent’s words.

### Additional explicit service and treatment requests

These include a request already made, a contemplated request for assessment, or a request for a change in treatment. Keep that distinction when using them as evidence of demand.

> I called the clinic back and requested my ultrasound results, which showed I am actually measuring at 5w5d almost three weeks behind what I estimated to be based on my menstrual cycle. Which makes sense because my scan at “4w5d” showed nothing, and my scan at “6w5d” only showed a gastrointestinal sac… so I’ve been measuring behind the entire time…
> 
> I also noticed it mentioned a SCH in the ultrasound comments that my doctor didn’t mention to me.
> 
> I’m so emotionally drained and scared to get my hopes up again. He sent a referral to the early pregnancy assessment clinic, and I’ll hear back tomorrow when I need to go in. According to the ultrasound paperwork, I should wait another week before having another ultrasound.
<!-- /verbatim -->

### Additional peer-experience, reassurance and interpretation questions

These further questions are kept distinct from the product/service asks: parents request outcomes after particular findings, describe symptoms, ask whether to worry or seek emotional company. They cannot be counted as willingness to buy a guide. Their IDs also appear in the thematic evidence or supporting lists above. This broader index preserves questions that sit between an experience request and a request for a practical plan.

### What the requests change

The current §6 says “These are the closest the corpus comes to asking for the product.” The new requests sharpen what the requested help actually is. The clearest requests in this share include books and preparations for postpartum mental health [pbu17-0271], baby-book recommendations as distraction [pbu22-0222], notes and an accompanied conversation after injury [pbu28-0118], a compact pregnancy pillow [pbu28-0332], how to enter prenatal care after a changed abortion decision [pbu28-0435], a more usable injection device [pbu35-0117], help obtaining an income while leaving [pbu35-0068], insurance advice without repeated sales calls [pbu35-0243], explanatory registry recommendations [pbu35-0361], and food ideas that stay tolerable [pbu35-0385]. Preserve the specific ask and its circumstances. These extend demand for usable help; they do not establish purchase intent for Mamivio or a price.

## Verification and coverage

Verification reads the four source files locally, extracts each delimited verbatim block, removes only its Markdown blockquote prefix, and compares the result for exact equality with the record’s complete `quote` field. It also checks every cited or anchor ID against this share and verifies the current-conclusion strings against `synthesis/findings.md`. Duplicated quotations are counted once for coverage; additional occurrences do not become additional evidence. Supporting lists preserve every record that was not reproduced.

Local verification passed: **1207 verbatim blocks** checked against complete source fields, representing **1100 distinct quote records**; **0 mismatches**, **0 unknown IDs**, **0 missing IDs**. All current-conclusion excerpts were found verbatim in the current findings. Every record in the money and specific-request indexes has its full source field reproduced in this digest. Anchor IDs are unique.

Coverage: quotes in the share **1486**; quotes reproduced verbatim **1100 distinct quote records**; further IDs listed without a full reproduction **386 distinct quote records**; IDs not mentioned at all **0**. Every quote record is represented; counts are quotes, not people.
